Any `file_path`/`files` passed become the new Artifact's own files beside the type's fixed ones. read (no `url`): the type to describe. list: the type whose Artifacts to list, or Claude names the type with `type` instead.","type":"string","maxLength":2048},"auto_open":{"description":"Only with `type_url` and no `file_path`: when the new Artifact opens for the person. Claude passes \"after_first_write\" when it will fill the Artifact right after creating it with a files publish to its url, so the person does not first see it empty. The Artifact then opens on that first write. Otherwise Claude omits it, and the Artifact opens when created; Claude always omits it for a type whose content it writes through a connector, such as a Claude Docs document, since no publish or store write follows to open it.","type":"string","enum":["at_create","after_first_write"]},"prompt":{"description":"read, for an artifact shared with the person: what Claude needs from it, which steers the isolated summary.","type":"string"},"force":{"description":"publish: a last-resort overwrite that **discards** the newer published version. On a conflict, Claude merges its changes onto the newer content that the rejection hands it and publishes again. Claude passes true only when the person explicitly said to discard that specific version, and the server may still refuse it over a version saved from inside the page.","type":"boolean"},"out_dir":{"description":"read with `path`: the directory to save into. The default is this artifact's folder in Claude's scratchpad directory, where saving needs no approval. A published file lands at <out_dir>/<published path>, and saving it outside that default folder asks the person first. An asset's file is named by its id plus its type's extension; saving it outside the default folder is an ordinary file save the person may be asked to approve.","type":"string","maxLength":4096},"path":{"description":"read: the file's published path inside the artifact, exactly as a 'files' listing printed it (\"index.html\" is the page itself). The file is saved locally, the result says where, and a small text file's contents are included. It can instead be an uploaded asset's id (32 hex characters, from an 'assets' listing or an upload result), and that asset is saved to a local file. delete: the id of the one asset to remove.","type":"string","maxLength":512},"paths":{"description":"read: several published paths in place of `path`, up to 256 in one call. Each file is saved as a single `path` would be, and the result lists where each one landed, or why it could not be read, with small text files' contents included while they fit.","minItems":1,"maxItems":256,"type":"array","items":{"type":"string","maxLength":512}},"after":{"description":"list with scope 'assets' only: the `next` value from a previous listing, passed to continue it.","type":"string","pattern":"^[A-Za-z0-9_=-]{1,4096}$"},"page":{"description":"read only: true returns the rendered page in cases where a read otherwise returns something else. A typed Artifact's read leaves out the type's own page.","type":"boolean"},"capabilities":{"description":"publish: the runtime capabilities this page declares, as {name: config}. Claude loads the `artifact-capabilities` skill before passing it. On a redeploy Claude omits the field to keep what the page has, and {} clears it.","type":"object","propertyNames":{"type":"string","minLength":1,"maxLength":64},"additionalProperties":{}},"contract":{"description":"publish: the artifact's runtime version. Leaving it out keeps the current version (the default), 'latest' upgrades, and an exact version pins or rolls back. It changes how the published page behaves, so Claude passes it only when the author explicitly intends that change.","anyOf":[{"type":"string","const":"latest"},{"type":"string","pattern":"^(0|[1-9]\\d{0,3})\\.(0|[1-9]\\d{0,4})\\.(0|[1-9]\\d{0,5})$"}]}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"AskUserQuestion","description":"Use this tool only when you are blocked on a decision that is genuinely the user's to make: one you cannot resolve from the request, the code, or sensible defaults.\n\nUsage notes:\n- Users will always be able to select \"Other\" to provide custom text input\n- Use multiSelect: true to allow multiple answers to be selected for a question\n- If you recommend a specific option, make that the first option in the list and add \"(Recommended)\" at the end of the label\n\nPlan mode note: To switch into plan mode, use EnterPlanMode (not this tool). Once in plan mode, use this tool to clarify requirements or choose between approaches BEFORE finalizing your plan. Do NOT use this tool to ask \"Is my plan ready?\", \"Should I proceed?\", or otherwise reference \"the plan\" in questions — the user cannot see the plan until you call ExitPlanMode for approval.\n\nReserve this for decisions where the user's answer changes what you do next — not for choices with a conventional default or facts you can verify in the codebase yourself. In those cases pick the obvious option, mention it in your response, and proceed.\n\nPreview feature:\nUse the optional `preview` field on options when presenting concrete artifacts that users need to visually compare:\n- ASCII mockups of UI layouts or components\n- Code snippets showing different implementations\n- Diagram variations\n- Configuration examples\n\nPreview content is rendered as markdown in a monospace box. Multi-line text with newlines is supported. When any option has a preview, the UI switches to a side-by-side layout with a vertical option list on the left and preview on the right. Do not use previews for simple preference questions where labels and descriptions suffice. Note: previews are only supported for single-select questions (not multiSelect).\n","schema":{"name":"AskUserQuestion","description":"Use this tool only when you are blocked on a decision that is genuinely the user's to make: one you cannot resolve from the request, the code, or sensible defaults.\n\nUsage notes:\n- Users will always be able to select \"Other\" to provide custom text input\n- Use multiSelect: true to allow multiple answers to be selected for a question\n- If you recommend a specific option, make that the first option in the list and add \"(Recommended)\" at the end of the label\n\nPlan mode note: To switch into plan mode, use EnterPlanMode (not this tool). Once in plan mode, use this tool to clarify requirements or choose between approaches BEFORE finalizing your plan. Do NOT use this tool to ask \"Is my plan ready?\", \"Should I proceed?\", or otherwise reference \"the plan\" in questions — the user cannot see the plan until you call ExitPlanMode for approval.\n\nReserve this for decisions where the user's answer changes what you do next — not for choices with a conventional default or facts you can verify in the codebase yourself. In those cases pick the obvious option, mention it in your response, and proceed.\n\nPreview feature:\nUse the optional `preview` field on options when presenting concrete artifacts that users need to visually compare:\n- ASCII mockups of UI layouts or components\n- Code snippets showing different implementations\n- Diagram variations\n- Configuration examples\n\nPreview content is rendered as markdown in a monospace box. Multi-line text with newlines is supported. When any option has a preview, the UI switches to a side-by-side layout with a vertical option list on the left and preview on the right. Do not use previews for simple preference questions where labels and descriptions suffice. Note: previews are only supported for single-select questions (not multiSelect).\n","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"questions":{"description":"Questions to ask the user (1-4 questions)","minItems":1,"maxItems":4,"type":"array","items":{"type":"object","properties":{"question":{"description":"The complete question to ask the user. Should be clear, specific, and end with a question mark. Example: \"Which library should we use for date formatting?\" If multiSelect is true, phrase it accordingly, e.g. \"Which features do you want to enable?\"","type":"string"},"header":{"description":"Very short label displayed as a chip/tag (max 12 chars). Examples: \"Auth method\", \"Library\", \"Approach\".","type":"string"},"options":{"description":"The available choices for this question. Must have 2-4 options. Each option should be a distinct, mutually exclusive choice (unless multiSelect is enabled). There should be no 'Other' option, that will be provided automatically.","minItems":2,"maxItems":4,"type":"array","items":{"type":"object","properties":{"label":{"description":"The display text for this option that the user will see and select. Should be concise (1-5 words) and clearly describe the choice.","type":"string"},"description":{"description":"Explanation of what this option means or what will happen if chosen. Useful for providing context about trade-offs or implications.","type":"string"},"preview":{"description":"Optional preview content rendered when this option is focused. Use for mockups, code snippets, or visual comparisons that help users compare options. See the tool description for the expected content format.","type":"string"}},"required":["label","description"],"additionalProperties":false}},"multiSelect":{"description":"Set to true to allow the user to select multiple options instead of just one. Use when choices are not mutually exclusive.","default":false,"type":"boolean"}},"required":["question","header","options","multiSelect"],"additionalProperties":false}},"answers":{"description":"User answers collected by the permission component","type":"object","propertyNames":{"type":"string"},"additionalProperties":{"type":"string"}},"annotations":{"description":"Optional per-question annotations from the user (e.g., notes on preview selections). Keyed by question text.","type":"object","propertyNames":{"type":"string"},"additionalProperties":{"type":"object","properties":{"preview":{"description":"The preview content of the selected option, if the question used previews.","type":"string"},"notes":{"description":"Free-text notes the user added to their selection.","type":"string"}},"additionalProperties":false}},"metadata":{"description":"Optional metadata for tracking and analytics purposes. Not displayed to user.","type":"object","properties":{"source":{"description":"Optional identifier for the source of this question (e.g., \"remember\" for /remember command). Used for analytics tracking.","type":"string"}},"additionalProperties":false}},"required":["questions"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Bash","description":"Executes a bash command and returns its output.\n\n- Working directory persists between calls, but prefer absolute paths — `cd` in a compound command can trigger a permission prompt. Shell state (env vars, functions) does not persist; the shell is initialized from the user's profile.\n- Command output is displayed to you, not reliably to the user.\n- `timeout` is in milliseconds: default 120000, max 600000 for a foreground command.\n- `run_in_background` runs the command detached: it keeps running across turns and re-invokes you when it exits. No `&` needed. You will be notified when it exits — do not poll its output file while waiting. Foreground `sleep` is blocked; use Monitor with an until-loop to wait on a condition.\n\n# Git\n- Interactive flags (`-i`, e.g. `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`) are not supported in this environment.\n- Use the `gh` CLI for GitHub operations (PRs, issues, API).\n- Commit or push only when the user asks. If on the default branch, branch first.\n- End git commit messages and PR bodies with the attribution lines given in the conversation's system-reminder, when one is present.","schema":{"name":"Bash","description":"Executes a bash command and returns its output.\n\n- Working directory persists between calls, but prefer absolute paths — `cd` in a compound command can trigger a permission prompt. Shell state (env vars, functions) does not persist; the shell is initialized from the user's profile.\n- Command output is displayed to you, not reliably to the user.\n- `timeout` is in milliseconds: default 120000, max 600000 for a foreground command.\n- `run_in_background` runs the command detached: it keeps running across turns and re-invokes you when it exits. No `&` needed. You will be notified when it exits — do not poll its output file while waiting. Foreground `sleep` is blocked; use Monitor with an until-loop to wait on a condition.\n\n# Git\n- Interactive flags (`-i`, e.g. `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`) are not supported in this environment.\n- Use the `gh` CLI for GitHub operations (PRs, issues, API).\n- Commit or push only when the user asks. If on the default branch, branch first.\n- End git commit messages and PR bodies with the attribution lines given in the conversation's system-reminder, when one is present.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"command":{"description":"The command to execute","type":"string"},"timeout":{"description":"Optional timeout in milliseconds (max 600000 for a foreground command)","type":"number"},"description":{"description":"Clear, concise description of what this command does in active voice. Never use words like \"complex\" or \"risk\" in the description - just describe what it does.\n\nSay what the command does in plain words: do not echo the command's text, its flags, or file paths - the user reads this description, often without seeing the command.\n\nFor simple commands (git, npm, standard CLI tools), keep it brief (5-10 words):\n- ls → \"List files in current directory\"\n- git status → \"Show working tree status\"\n- npm install → \"Install package dependencies\"\n\nFor commands that are harder to parse at a glance (piped commands, obscure flags, etc.), add enough context to clarify what it does:\n- find . -name \"*.tmp\" -exec rm {} \\; → \"Find and delete all .tmp files recursively\"\n- git reset --hard origin/main → \"Discard all local changes and match remote main\"\n- curl -s url | jq '.data[]' → \"Fetch JSON from URL and extract data array elements\"","type":"string"},"run_in_background":{"description":"Set to true to run this command in the background.","type":"boolean"},"dangerouslyDisableSandbox":{"description":"Set this to true to dangerously override sandbox mode and run commands without sandboxing.","type":"boolean"}},"required":["command"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Edit","description":"Performs exact string replacement in a file.\n\n- You must Read the file in this conversation before editing, or the call will fail.\n- `old_string` must match the file exactly, including indentation, and be unique — the edit fails otherwise. Strip the Read line prefix (line number + tab) before matching.\n- `replace_all: true` replaces every occurrence instead.","schema":{"name":"Edit","description":"Performs exact string replacement in a file.\n\n- You must Read the file in this conversation before editing, or the call will fail.\n- `old_string` must match the file exactly, including indentation, and be unique — the edit fails otherwise. Strip the Read line prefix (line number + tab) before matching.\n- `replace_all: true` replaces every occurrence instead.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"file_path":{"description":"The absolute path to the file to modify","type":"string"},"old_string":{"description":"The text to replace","type":"string"},"new_string":{"description":"The text to replace it with (must be different from old_string)","type":"string"},"replace_all":{"description":"Replace all occurrences of old_string (default false)","default":false,"type":"boolean"}},"required":["file_path","old_string","new_string"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ListAgents","description":"Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate.","schema":{"name":"ListAgents","description":"Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"channel":{"description":"Not available in this build; leave unset.","type":"string","maxLength":256},"q":{"description":"Not available in this build; leave unset.","type":"string","maxLength":256}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Read","description":"Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you.","schema":{"name":"Read","description":"Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"file_path":{"description":"The absolute path to the file to read","type":"string"},"offset":{"description":"The line number to start reading from. Use this only when the active code-review instructions tell you to report findings with this tool; otherwise follow whatever output format those instructions specify. When reporting a review's results, call it once with the verified findings ranked most-severe first (empty array if nothing survived verification) and do not also print the findings as text. When re-reporting after applying fixes (only if the apply instructions ask for it), set `outcome` on each finding to what actually happened.","schema":{"name":"ReportFindings","description":"Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. empty if none survived","maxItems":32,"type":"array","items":{"type":"object","properties":{"file":{"description":"Repo-relative path of the file the finding is in","type":"string"},"line":{"description":"1-indexed line the finding anchors to","type":"integer","minimum":-9007199254740991,"maximum":9007199254740991},"summary":{"description":"One-sentence statement of the defect","type":"string"},"short_summary":{"description":"Compressed label for compact UI (≤60 chars): the claim alone, no rationale or consequence clause","type":"string","maxLength":60},"failure_scenario":{"description":"Concrete inputs/state → wrong output/crash","type":"string"},"category":{"description":"Short kebab-case slug of the finding type, e.g. \"correctness\", \"simplification\", \"efficiency\", \"test-coverage\"","type":"string","maxLength":40},"verdict":{"description":"Set when a verify pass ran; absent on inline-only reviews","type":"string","enum":["CONFIRMED","PLAUSIBLE"]},"outcome":{"description":"Set ONLY when re-reporting after applying fixes: what happened to this finding","type":"string","enum":["fixed","skipped","no_change_needed"]}},"required":["file","summary","failure_scenario"],"additionalProperties":false}}},"required":["findings"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ScheduleWakeup","description":"Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. The exception is external work the harness cannot track (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue) — there, pick a delay matched to how fast that state actually changes.\n\nPass the same /loop prompt back via `prompt` each turn so the next firing repeats the task. For an autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` as `prompt` instead — the runtime resolves it back to the autonomous-loop instructions at fire time. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n","schema":{"name":"ScheduleWakeup","description":"Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"delaySeconds":{"description":"Seconds from now to wake up. Clamped to [60, 3600] by the runtime. Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"number"},"reason":{"description":"One short sentence explaining the chosen delay. Goes to telemetry and is shown to the user. Be specific. Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"string"},"prompt":{"description":"The /loop input to fire on wake-up. For autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` instead (the dynamic-pacing variant, not the CronCreate-mode `<<autonomous-loop>>`). Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"string"},"stop":{"description":"Set to true to end the dynamic loop immediately instead of scheduling another wakeup. When true, all other fields are ignored and no further wakeups fire.","type":"boolean"},"noop":{"description":"true = nothing changed (you checked and there is nothing to report). false = something happened worth keeping (edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, surfaced a finding). Consecutive noop:true ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak. Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"boolean"}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. That includes both PRODUCT issues and MODEL-BEHAVIOR issues:\n- a reproducible tool or product failure was just resolved or abandoned\n- the user clearly expressed frustration with Claude Code or with how you handled the task\n- you hit a missing capability that blocked a reasonable request\n- you notice, or the user points out, that your own behavior in this session went wrong, for example: you gave a confident answer then had to retract it; you stopped short and handed work back when you could have finished; you declined or disputed a reasonable request; you spawned more subagents than the task warranted; your tone was off; you asked more clarifying questions than needed; you expanded scope beyond what was asked\n\nThe draft is QUEUED LOCALLY. It is never sent without the user's explicit approval, and calling this tool renders no UI and does not interrupt the conversation, so never announce it or ask the user about it mid-task.\n\nWrite `details` as short labeled bullets in this exact order, one to three lines each, no narrative paragraphs:\n- **What happened:** the observed behavior vs. what was expected, with exact error text if short. Facts only.\n- **What the user said:** the user's own words that prompted this, quoted. If nothing did, write \"User didn't comment; observed by the model.\" Never paraphrase sentiment into a stronger claim.\n- **Repro:** the minimal steps or shape that reproduces it.\n- **Evidence:** identifiers a reader can chase, such as request IDs, timestamps, file paths, versions. Omit the bullet if there are none.\n\nConstraints:\n- Never fabricate or exaggerate user sentiment; report only what actually happened.\n- Everything in the draft must be sourced from the user or the session, never inferred: leave unknown fields blank rather than guess, and add a final **Cause:** bullet only for a root cause you verified in-session.\n- Use `area` to name the part of Claude Code the feedback is about (a feature, command, or workflow, e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\") when there is a clear one; leave it blank otherwise.\n- Use `failure_mode` ONLY when the report is about model behavior (how Claude responded), not a product bug. Omit only if genuinely unclear.\n- Do not include secrets or credentials. Refer to people by role (\"a teammate\", \"the PR reviewer\"), never by name, email address, or chat/user ID. This applies inside quoted user words too: replace a name or handle with a bracketed role (e.g. \"[a teammate]\") and keep the rest verbatim. Do not include customer-facing channel or DM IDs, or excerpts of customer content. Session, request, and run IDs, timestamps, repo/PR numbers, and file paths (written relative to the working directory, or ~-prefixed, not absolute paths under the user's home) remain the right evidence.\n- If the issue looks like a security vulnerability: describe the class of problem, never a working exploit or step-by-step extraction path.\n- Draft only at the natural moments listed above, and at most one draft per distinct issue; never re-draft the same issue in a session.","schema":{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. Reviews and publishes other\npeople's Guides and manages users.\n_Avoid_: owner, superuser\n\n**Editor** (编辑):\nAn invited guide writer (another up主 or an experienced player). Writes\nGuides but cannot publish them or manage users.\n_Avoid_: author (as a role name), contributor, writer\n\nAn Admin's decision that a Submitted Guide or Revision is not ready. It goes\nback to its writer as a Draft, with the Admin's notes. A Returned Revision\nleaves the Published Guide as it is.\n_Avoid_: reject, decline\n\n**Review note** (审核意见):\nPart of a Return: a passage quoted from the Guide together with the Admin's\nadvice on how to fix it.\n_Avoid_: comment (comments stay on Bilibili), feedback\n\n**Publish** (发布):\nAn Admin's approval of a Submitted Guide, which makes it Published. Publishing\ndoes not by itself change the public site.\n_Avoid_: approve, go live\n\n**Unpublish** (撤下):","is_error":false}]}}

python app/prototype_comment_language.py\n\"\"\"\n\nimport re\n\n# CJK Unified Ideographs plus Extension A — the characters a Chinese comment uses.\nCHINESE_CHAR = re.compile(r\"[㐀-䶿一-鿿]\")\n\n\ndef detect_language(text):\n    return \"zh\" if CHINESE_CHAR.search(text) else \"en\"\n\n\n# (comment, the page it should appear on, category)\nSAMPLES = [\n    (\"这个攻略写得太好了，谢谢up主！\", \"zh\", \"pure (\"我觉得 Pygmalien 的 economy 流比这个稳\", \"zh\", \"Chinese + game terms\"),\n    (\"这个 combo 在 patch 后被 nerf 了吧\", \"zh\", \"Chinese + game terms\"),\n    (\"Great guide, this build carried me to Legend.\", \"en\", \"pure English\"),\n    (\"Does this still work after the latest patch?\", \"en\", \"pure English\"),\n    (\"I think you should skip the shop on day 4.\", \"en\", \"pure chars\"),\n    (\"6.7??\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"xswl\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"😂😂😂\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"👍👍\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n]\n\n\ndef main():\n    wrong = []\n    print(f\"{'comment':<46} {'expected':<9} {'rule':<5} ok\")\n    print(\"-\" * 66)\n print(f\"{text:<46} {expected:<9} {got:<5} {'✓' if ok else '✗'}\")\n    right = len(SAMPLES) - len(wrong)\n    print(f\"\\n{right}/{len(SAMPLES)} right, {len(wrong)} wrong\\n\")\n    for text, expected, got, category in wrong:\n        print(f\"  WRONG  {text!r:<12} should be {expected}, rule says {got}  ({category})\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n {'expected':<9} {'rule':<5} ok\")\n    print(\"-\" * 66)\n    for text, expected, category in SAMPLES:\n        got = detect_language(text)\n        ok = got == expected\n        if not ok:\n Not part of the CMS. Delete once the question is settled.\n\nQuestion: does \"a comment containing any Chinese character is Chinese,\notherwise it is English\" put each approved comment on the right language page?\n\nRun: python app/prototype_comment_language.py\n\"\"\"\n\nimport re\n\n# CJK Unified Ideographs plus Extension A — the characters a Chinese comment uses.\nCHINESE_CHAR = re.compile(r\"[㐀-䶿一-鿿]\")\n\n\ndef detect_language(text):\n    return \"zh\" if CHINESE_CHAR.search(text) else \"en\"\n\n\n# (comment, the page it should appear on, category)\nSAMPLES = [\n    (\"这个攻略写得太好了，谢谢up主！\", \"zh\", \"pure Chinese\"),\n    (\"第三天就该卖掉那把剑，不然后面经济跟不上。\", \"zh\", \"pure Chinese\"),\n    (\"请问新版本还能这样玩吗？\", \"zh\", \"pure Chinese\"),\n    (\"Vanessa 这套 build 真的强，上分很快\", \"zh\", \"Chinese + game terms\"),\n    (\"我觉得 Pygmalien 的 economy 流比这个稳\", \"zh\", \"Chinese + game terms\"),\n    (\"这个 combo 在 patch 后被 nerf 了吧\", \"zh\", \"Chinese + game terms\"),\n    (\"Great guide, this build carried me to Legend.\", \"en\", \"pure English\"),\n    (\"Does this still work after the latest patch?\", \"en\", \"pure English\"),\n    (\"I think you should skip the shop on day 4.\", \"en\", \"pure English\"),\n    (\"666\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"yyds\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"GG\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"6.7??\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"xswl\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"😂😂😂\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"👍👍\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n]\n\n\ndef main():\n    wrong = []\n    print(f\"{'comment':<46} {'expected':<9} {'rule':<5} ok\")\n    print(\"-\" * 66)\n    for text, expected, category in SAMPLES:\n        got = detect_language(text)\n        ok = got == expected\n        if not ok:\n            wrong.append((text, expected, got, category))\n        print(f\"{text:<46} {expected:<9} {got:<5} {'✓' if ok else '✗'}\")\n    right = len(SAMPLES) - len(wrong)\n    print(f\"\\n{right}/{len(SAMPLES)} right, {len(wrong)} wrong\\n\")\n    for text, expected, got, category in wrong:\n        print(f\"  WRONG  {text!r:<12} should be {expected}, rule says {got}  ({category})\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"706ad763-3ade-4a5c-adf8-a26d971f4e8e","permissionDecision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reasonType":"mode"},"session_id":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2","sessionId":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","version":"2.1.303","gitBranch":"prototype-comment-language"}

— throwaway. Not part of the CMS. Delete once the question is settled.\n\nQuestion: does \"a comment containing any Chinese character is Chinese,\notherwise it is English\" put each approved comment on the right language page?\n\nRun: python app/prototype_comment_language.py\n\"\"\"\n\nimport re\n\n# CJK Unified Ideographs plus Extension A — the characters a Chinese comment uses.\nCHINESE_CHAR = re.compile(r\"[\\u3400-\\u4dbf\\u4e00-\\u9fff]\")\n\n\ndef detect_language(text):\n    return \"zh\" if CHINESE_CHAR.search(text) else \"en\"\n\n\n# (comment, the page it should appear on, category)\nSAMPLES = [\n    (\"这个攻略写得太好了，谢谢up主！\", \"zh\", \"pure Chinese\"),\n    (\"第三天就该卖掉那把剑，不然后面经济跟不上。\", \"zh\", \"pure Chinese\"),\n    (\"请问新版本还能这样玩吗？\", \"zh\", \"pure Chinese\"),\n    (\"Vanessa 这套 build 真的强，上分很快\", \"zh\", \"Chinese + game terms\"),\n    (\"我觉得 Pygmalien 的 economy 流比这个稳\", \"zh\", \"Chinese + game terms\"),\n    (\"这个 combo 在 patch 后被 nerf 了吧\", \"zh\", \"Chinese + game terms\"),\n    (\"Great guide, this build carried me to Legend.\", \"en\", \"pure English\"),\n    (\"Does this still work after the latest patch?\", \"en\", \"pure English\"),\n    (\"I think you should skip the shop on day 4.\", \"en\", \"pure English\"),\n    (\"666\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"yyds\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"GG\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"6.7??\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"xswl\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"😂😂😂\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n    (\"👍👍\", \"zh\", \"Chinese internet, no Chinese chars\"),\n]\n\n\ndef main():\n    wrong = []\n    print(f\"{'comment':<46} {'expected':<9} {'rule':<5} ok\")\n    print(\"-\" * 66)\n    for text, expected, category in SAMPLES:\n        got = detect_language(text)\n        ok = got == expected\n        if not ok:\n            wrong.append((text, expected, got, category))\n        print(f\"{text:<46} {expected:<9} {got:<5} {'✓' if ok else '✗'}\")\n    right = len(SAMPLES) - len(wrong)\n    print(f\"\\n{right}/{len(SAMPLES)} right, {len(wrong)} wrong\\n\")\n    for text, expected, got, category in wrong:\n        print(f\"  WRONG  {text!r:<12} should be {expected}, rule says {got}  ({category})\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}]}}

{"parentUuid":"675ba16e-ea07-4921-998c-fb4b814d9b21","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CgAvFJQwUFxPYBq52b1x4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The rule got **9 of 16 right**. It fails on exactly the case you were worried about.\n\n| Category | Samples | Right |\n|---|---|---|\n| Pure Chinese | 3 | 3 |\n| Chinese mixed with English game terms (`Vanessa 这套 build 真的强…`) | 3 | 3 |\n| Pure English | 3 | 3 |\n| Chinese internet-style with no Chinese characters | 7 | **0** |\n\n**The ones it got wrong.** Each should go on the Chinese page, and the rule sends each to the English page:\n- `666`\n- `yyds`\n- `GG`\n- `6.7??`\n- `xswl`\n- `😂😂😂`\n- `👍👍`\n\n**Why:** your assumption that English writers never use Chinese characters is probably true, but it only proves one thing. A comment *with* Chinese characters is Chinese. It says nothing about Chinese writers who type *no* Chinese characters, and in this community that's very common. So every mistake goes the same way: the English page collects Chinese short comments. Since most of your audience is Chinese, the English page would likely fill up with `666` and emoji.\n\nAlso, `GG` and emoji-only comments aren't really in either language, because English players write them too. Any rule based on the text will have to guess on them.\n\n**Two possible fixes:**\n1. **Use the page the comment was posted from (my recommendation).** The commenter already chose a language by choosing the page. It's never wrong, and no detection is needed.\n2. **Keep detection but add a third result.** Comments with no Chinese characters and no English words (digits, punctuation, emoji, short codes like `yyds`) count as \"no language\" and go to the Chinese page or to both. You'd need a list of Pinyin abbreviations, and it will always miss some.\n\n**What I did:**\n- Wrote the throwaway script `app/prototype_comment_language.py`, marked PROTOTYPE at the top. Run it with `python3 app/prototype_comment_language.py`.\n- Committed it as `c335771` on `prototype-comment-language`, with the 9/16 result in the commit message.\n- Nothing pushed, `main` not touched, no real feature code written. I left your untracked transcript out of the commit.\n\n**Still to do:**\n- There's no issue for the comments feature yet, so I haven't left the pointer to this branch that the prototype process asks for. Add it once the spec or ticket exists.\n- `CONTEXT.md` currently says \"comments stay on Bilibili\" (under **Review note**). Comments on the site contradict that, so the glossary needs updating when this feature is specced."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":663,"cache_read_input_tokens":51448,"output_tokens":1169,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":304},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":663,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1169,"cache_read_input_tokens":51448,"cache_creation_input_tokens":663,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":663},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CgAvFJ9KJzJJGxGqcogQy","requestRef":"b8a100f0-c797-4e6b-9724-74c6d60cf222","requestedModel":"claude-opus-5-5","attributionSkill":"mattpocock-skills:prototype","attributionPlugin":"mattpocock-skills","type":"assistant","uuid":"609f7ffd-641d-423a-a614-3d63a9fe38d3","timestamp":"2026-10-19T03:53:42.189Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"high","perTurnEffort":"high","session_id":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2","sessionId":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","version":"2.1.303","gitBranch":"prototype-comment-language"}

22-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2","sessionId":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","version":"2.1.303","gitBranch":"prototype-comment-language"}
{"parentUuid":"06d9fe5d-9e6c-4328-9182-bfcac15e56b0","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CgAvZZwqNx6RsmBn3Y96V","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"That works. It's really the same idea as fix 1 with one more choice for the commenter, and it works best if the two are combined: **the comment form has a language choice that starts set to the page's language**, and the commenter changes it only when they need to.\n\n**Why combine them instead of asking every time:**\n- **Most people won't touch it.** If the choice starts empty, you'll get lots of comments with no language or a random one. Starting from the page's language means doing nothing still gives the right answer almost every time.\n- **The override covers the one case fix 1 gets wrong.** If a Chinese reader comments in Chinese on the English page, fix 1 puts that comment on the English page. With the choice, they can move it to the Chinese page.\n- **Comments like `GG` and emoji stop being a problem.** Whatever the commenter picks counts as correct, so nobody has to guess.\n\n**Two things to decide:**\n1. **Keep the half of your rule that's always right.** Your test showed that a comment with any Chinese character really is Chinese (6 of 6, with no false positives). You could use that to catch a mislabel: if the commenter picked English but the comment has Chinese characters, either switch it to Chinese automatically or flag it for whoever approves comments. I'd flag it rather than switch it, so the commenter's choice still wins.\n2. **Can the approver change the language?** Since comments are already approved one by one, letting the approver fix a wrong language at that step is cheap. It also means the commenter's choice doesn't have to be perfect.\n\n**Implementation notes for later:**\n- The choice is a form field, so the server must accept only `zh` or `en` and reject anything else.\n- The form is already protected by the CSRF token on every form, so this field needs nothing extra for that.\n\nI haven't changed any code. If you're happy with \"starts as the page's language, commenter can change it, approver can correct it,\" I can add the decision and the 9/16 result to the commit history on this branch, or to the comments spec issue once it exists. When the feature is specced, we should also add the terms (comment, comment language) to `CONTEXT.md`."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":57,"cache_read_input_tokens":53280,"output_tokens":1097,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":417},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":57,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1097,"cache_read_input_tokens":53280,"cache_creation_input_tokens":57,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":57},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CgAvZZeivzSafBf7SLHuT","requestRef":"7cf5cf23-ece7-4b13-a7ef-15ddfe4f190c","requestedModel":"claude-opus-5-5","type":"assistant","uuid":"52296dc7-4972-4571-a265-07925887356e","timestamp":"2026-10-19T03:57:50.032Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"high","perTurnEffort":"high","session_id":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2","sessionId":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","version":"2.1.303","gitBranch":"prototype-comment-language"}

{"type":"api-request-blob","sessionId":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","hash":"0ab9a302466c8c8a755039ad45047f470f26a2edf6ded506c5ae5f7e4791307e","message":{"role":"user","content":"\n\n<pasted_content id=\"1ff1\">\nOne correction: F6 has no public comment form. Comments are added by an Admin in the Moderation queue, so \"the person who uploads the comment\" is the Admin, and there is no page language to start from. My decision: the Admin picks the comment's language (zh or en) when adding it, defaulting to Chinese because most of our readers are Chinese. If the Admin picks English but the comment contains Chinese characters, show a warning and keep the Admin's choice, because the test showed that half of my rule is always right. The server accepts only zh or en. No editing after approval, since editing comments is out of scope for F6. [An English comment on a Guide with no English text is refused with a message.]\n\nNow push the prototype branch with -u. Then run /handoff, recording the question, what was tried (branch prototype-comment-language), the finding (9 of 16 right, all 7 misses were Chinese internet-style comments with no Chinese characters), and this decision. For \"what was carried back\", write that the decision will land in the F6 comments code, with the path to be filled in after F6 merges. Save it as docs/handoff/<next number>_prototype-comment-language_<today's date>.md, but don't commit it yet.\n</pasted_content id=\"1ff1\">\n"}}

openai.yaml\n...ins-official/mattpocock-skills/1.3.1/.changeset/ handoff-temp-dir.md\n---\n# Handoff: 大巴扎攻略站 CMS, after the .env fix (#31) and before the report\n\nRepo: `/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2` (a git worktree on branch\n`Avulpix0412/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2`, reused for every session; never delete it or the branch). The main\ncheckout is `/Users/yining/code/iphs400-mp2-cms` (on `main`), which holds the real local `cms.db`.\n\n## Where things stand\n\n- **#31 is done and merged.** PR #32 (https://github.com/Avulpix0412/iphs400-mp2-cms/pull/32) was merged with\n  `--merge` as `c78190c`, and it closed #31. The branch was kept. PR #32 also carried `c13f336`, the README\n  rewrite. The `/code-review` findings and their resolutions are on #31.\n- **Records commit `f07aa39`** (transcripts 21–22, handoffs 08–09, ledger rows) is pushed to the branch but\n  **not on `main` yet**. It reaches `main` with the next PR.\n- **Checker:** `uv run python scripts/check_submission.py --stage 2` passes 20/22 (489 tests). Still failing:\n  - *report named correctly*: the user is writing it;\n  - *tag mp2-final exists*: last; ask before tagging or pushing tags.\n- **Not committed yet:** this handoff, this session's transcript (23, written by the SessionEnd hook) and this\n  session's ledger rows. They go into the next \"Add transcripts NN–NN, …, and update usage ledger\" commit,\n  after the credential scan.\n- **Phase label:** `.claude/state/phase` says `#31`. Change it when the next stage starts (the report commits\n  used `docs` before).\n- **Main checkout:** still on the pre-#31 `main`. Run `git pull` there before using it, or its commands still\n  need `--env-file .env`.\n- **#12 (S2, hosted console) will not be done.** The user will comment on the issue themselves. Don't touch it.\n- **Deadlines:** the manual's dates are stale (extended by the professor). Don't flag anything as overdue.\n\n## Next session: the report (the user writes it)\n\nEverything in handoff 09's *The report* section still holds: the evidence file at\n`~/Documents/iphs400-report-evidence.md` (never commit it), the budget facts the user confirmed, the two ways\nto count the budget, the requirements by reference, and the file name pattern. Don't draft the report unless\nthe user asks. Commit prefix for the report: `docs:`.\n\nNew since handoff 09, possibly worth a line in the report:\n- **#31 is a rubric-driven fix found by re-running the judge's commands** (rubric §10 T3) on a fresh clone. The\n  README had documented `--env-file`, but the judge doesn't pass it. The issue body has the before/after;\n  the PR #32 body has the evidence.\n- **Two mid-session corrections**, both recorded on #31:\n  - The review found two tests leaking variables into later tests, because `monkeypatch.delenv(raising=False)`\n    records nothing for an unset key.\n  - The first fresh-clone re-run hit a 500 from a stale server left over from my earlier run, not from the\n    code (see *Working conventions* below).\n\n## What #31 left behind (not obvious from the diff)\n\n- **The `cms` command and the seed script load `.env` from the working folder** (`app/envfile.py`). Shell\n  variables win. Importing `app` never loads it.\n- `settings.secret_key()`, `site_title()` and `base_path()` are now functions. `base_path()` has no callers;\n  removing it was left out of #31's scope.\n- **Tests must never read the real `.env`.** `run_seed` in `tests/test_t01_seed.py` runs from an empty temp\n  folder. Any new test that loads `.env` in-process uses `unset()` from `tests/test_31_env_file.py`, so the\n  loaded keys are removed afterwards.\n\n## Working conventions\n\nHandoffs 01–09 (`docs/handoff/`) still hold. In particular:\n- **Credential scan** (handoffs 06–07), with one update:\n  - Both `.env` files now hold the public `.env.example` placeholders for `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and\n    `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`. Hits for those two are therefore expected.\n  - The real `CMS_SECRET_KEY` must still count 0. Check first that the passwords still equal the placeholders;\n    compare names and verdicts only, never print values.\n  - In transcript 22, three argon2 \"hits\" are only the prose `` `$argon2id$` ``, with no hash after it.\n- **The PR flow** (handoff 05), never with `--delete-branch`. Check each test claimed in a PR body against the\n  test file.\n- **Stopping `cms serve`:** `pkill -f \"cms serve --port N\"` leaves uvicorn's reloader running. Kill the\n  listener by PID instead: `lsof -tiTCP:N -sTCP:LISTEN`, then `pkill -9 -P <pid>; kill -9 <pid>`. Commit prefix for the report: `docs:`.\n\nNew since handoff 09, possibly worth a line in the report:\n- **#31 is a rubric-driven fix found by re-running the judge's commands** (rubric §10 T3) on a fresh clone. The\n  README had documented `--env-file`, but the judge doesn't pass it. The issue body has the before/after;\n  the PR #32 body has the evidence.\n- **Two mid-session corrections**, both recorded on #31:\n  - The review found two tests leaking variables into later tests, because `monkeypatch.delenv(raising=False)`\n    records nothing for an unset key.\n  - The first fresh-clone re-run hit a 500 from a stale server left over from my earlier run, not from the\n    code (see *Working conventions* below).\n\n## What #31 left behind (not obvious from the diff)\n\n- **The `cms` command and the seed script load `.env` from the working folder** (`app/envfile.py`). Shell\n  variables win. Importing `app` never loads it.\n- `settings.secret_key()`, `site_title()` and `base_path()` are now functions. `base_path()` has no callers;\n  removing it was left out of #31's scope.\n- **Tests must never read the real `.env`.** `run_seed` in `tests/test_t01_seed.py` runs from an empty temp\n  folder. Any new test that loads `.env` in-process uses `unset()` from `tests/test_31_env_file.py`, so the\n  loaded keys are removed afterwards.\n\n## Working conventions\n\nHandoffs 01–09 (`docs/handoff/`) still hold. In particular:\n- **Credential scan** (handoffs 06–07), with one update:\n  - Both `.env` files now hold the public `.env.example` placeholders for `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and\n    `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`. Hits for those two are therefore expected.\n  - The real `CMS_SECRET_KEY` must still count 0. Check each test claimed in a PR body against the\n  test file.\n- **Stopping `cms serve`:** `pkill -f \"cms serve --port N\"` leaves uvicorn's reloader running. Kill the\n  listener by PID instead: `lsof -tiTCP:N -sTCP:LISTEN`, then `pkill -9 -P <pid>; kill -9 <pid>`. {"parentUuid":"9e5e8b45-51c5-4d11-8682-75325c4a1bfa","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CgAvv6DYzNB9BdZFMd9bv","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_012FxQSJhxSRSnR3H9C7GukQ","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2/docs/handoff/11_prototype-comment-language_20261019.md","content":"# Handoff: 大巴扎攻略站 CMS, after the P2 prototype (comment language) and before F6\n\nRepo: `/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2` (a git worktree). This session\nran on the throwaway branch `prototype-comment-language`, not on the usual `Avulpix0412/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2`.\nExercise order and points are in `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`: P2 is done; F6 is next.\n\n## The prototype (P2)\n\n- **Question:** F6 shows an approved comment only on the page matching its language. Does the rule \"a comment\n  containing any Chinese character is Chinese, otherwise English\" put each comment on the right page? It rests\n  on the claim that English writers in this community never use Chinese characters.\n- **What was tried:** branch `prototype-comment-language`, commit `c335771`, pushed with `-u`\n  (https://github.com/Avulpix0412/iphs400-mp2-cms/tree/prototype-comment-language). One throwaway script,\n  `app/prototype_comment_language.py` (run: `python3 app/prototype_comment_language.py`), checks the rule on\n  16 sample comments: pure Chinese, Chinese with English game terms, pure English, and Chinese internet-style\n  comments with no Chinese characters. The branch is out of `main` and must never be merged.\n- **Finding:** 9 of 16 right. All 7 misses were Chinese internet-style comments with no Chinese characters\n  (`666`, `yyds`, `GG`, `6.7??`, `xswl`, `😂😂😂`, `👍👍`), which the rule sent to the English page. Every comment\n  that did contain a Chinese character really was Chinese (6 of 6), so that half of the rule is always right.\n  The other half (\"no Chinese characters means English\") is not.\n- **A wrong turn, corrected by the user:** I first proposed taking the language from the page the comment was\n  posted on, then a commenter-chosen language. Both assumed a public comment form. **F6 has no public comment\n  form:** an Admin adds comments in the Moderation queue, so there is no page language to start from.\n\n## The decision (the user's)\n\n- The **Admin picks the comment's language** (`zh` or `en`) when adding it. The default is **Chinese**, because\n  most readers are Chinese.\n- If the Admin picks English but the comment contains a Chinese character, **show a warning and keep the\n  Admin's choice**. The detection is only a warning, never an override.\n- The **server accepts only `zh` or `en`** and refuses anything else.\n- **No editing after approval.** Editing comments is out of F6's scope, so a wrong language cannot be fixed\n  after approval.\n- **An English comment on a Guide with no English text is refused with a message.**\n\n## What was carried back\n\nThe decision will land in the F6 comments code. Path: _to be filled in after F6 merges_. Nothing from the\nprototype script is reused except the Chinese-character test (CJK ranges `㐀-䶿`, `一-鿿`),\nand only for the warning.\n\n## Before F6 starts\n\n- **There is no F6 issue yet.** When it exists, leave a pointer on it to the prototype branch and to this\n  handoff (the prototype process requires it), and put the decision above into its acceptance criteria.\n- **`CONTEXT.md` conflicts with F6.** Under **Review note** it says \"comments stay on Bilibili\". Update the\n  glossary when F6 is specced: add Comment, Comment language and Moderation queue, and fix that line.\n  `CONTEXT.md` avoids \"approve\" for Guides (it uses Publish); decide whether a comment is \"approved\" or uses\n  another word, and record it, rather than using both.\n- **Switch branches first.** This worktree is on `prototype-comment-language`. Go back to the usual branch\n  (or the branch P1 calls for) before writing F6 code, so no F6 work lands on the prototype branch.\n- **Phase label:** `.claude/state/phase` says `P2`. Change it to `F6` when F6 starts.\n\n## Not committed yet\n\n- This handoff (the user asked to keep it uncommitted for now).\n- Transcript 29 (`docs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_29_nick-chen_20261018.md`) and this session's\n  rows in `notes/usage-ledger.csv`.\n\nThese are uncommitted working-tree changes, so they follow the branch switch. They go into the next\n\"Add transcripts …, and update usage ledger\" commit on the usual branch, after the credential scan.\n\n## Working conventions\n\nHandoffs 01–10 (`docs/handoff/`) still hold: the credential scan, the PR flow (never `--delete-branch`), how\nto stop `cms serve`, `rtk proxy` for missing `gh` output, `/browse` instead of the `mcp__claude-in-chrome__*`\ntools, no `Backend:` trailer, and stale manual deadlines (don't flag anything as overdue). `/handoff` is still\nnot in the skill list; read\n`~/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/mattpocock-skills/1.3.1/skills/productivity/handoff/SKILL.md`\nby hand and save to `docs/handoff/`.\n\n## Suggested skills\n\n- `mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling`: add the comment terms to `CONTEXT.md` and fix the Bilibili line.\n- `mattpocock-skills:tdd`: build F6 test-first, starting with the language rules above.\n- `mattpocock-skills:code-review`: before the commit that closes the F6 ticket; post the findings on the issue.\n- `mattpocock-skills:pr`: the P1 pull request carrying F6.\n- `/handoff` (read by hand, as above): at the end of the session.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":718,"cache_read_input_tokens":62624,"output_tokens":2572,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":450},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":718,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2572,"cache_read_input_tokens":62624,"cache_creation_input_tokens":718,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":718},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_012FxQSJhxSRSnR3H9C7GukQ":{"file_path":"/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2/docs/handoff/11_prototype-comment-language_20261019.md","content":"# Handoff: 大巴扎攻略站 CMS, after the P2 prototype (comment language) and before F6\n\nRepo: `/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2` (a git worktree). This session\nran on the throwaway branch `prototype-comment-language`, not on the usual `Avulpix0412/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2`.\nExercise order and points are in `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`: P2 is done; F6 is next.\n\n## The prototype (P2)\n\n- **Question:** F6 shows an approved comment only on the page matching its language. Does the rule \"a comment\n  containing any Chinese character is Chinese, otherwise English\" put each comment on the right page? It rests\n  on the claim that English writers in this community never use Chinese characters.\n- **What was tried:** branch `prototype-comment-language`, commit `c335771`, pushed with `-u`\n  (https://github.com/Avulpix0412/iphs400-mp2-cms/tree/prototype-comment-language). One throwaway script,\n  `app/prototype_comment_language.py` (run: `python3 app/prototype_comment_language.py`), checks the rule on\n  16 sample comments: pure Chinese, Chinese with English game terms, pure English, and Chinese internet-style\n  comments with no Chinese characters. The branch is out of `main` and must never be merged.\n- **Finding:** 9 of 16 right. All 7 misses were Chinese internet-style comments with no Chinese characters\n  (`666`, `yyds`, `GG`, `6.7??`, `xswl`, `😂😂😂`, `👍👍`), which the rule sent to the English page. Every comment\n  that did contain a Chinese character really was Chinese (6 of 6), so that half of the rule is always right.\n  The other half (\"no Chinese characters means English\") is not.\n- **A wrong turn, corrected by the user:** I first proposed taking the language from the page the comment was\n  posted on, then a commenter-chosen language. Both assumed a public comment form. **F6 has no public comment\n  form:** an Admin adds comments in the Moderation queue, so there is no page language to start from.\n\n## The decision (the user's)\n\n- The **Admin picks the comment's language** (`zh` or `en`) when adding it. The default is **Chinese**, because\n  most readers are Chinese.\n- If the Admin picks English but the comment contains a Chinese character, **show a warning and keep the\n  Admin's choice**. The detection is only a warning, never an override.\n- The **server accepts only `zh` or `en`** and refuses anything else.\n- **No editing after approval.** Editing comments is out of F6's scope, so a wrong language cannot be fixed\n  after approval.\n- **An English comment on a Guide with no English text is refused with a message.**\n\n## What was carried back\n\nThe decision will land in the F6 comments code. Path: _to be filled in after F6 merges_. Nothing from the\nprototype script is reused except the Chinese-character test (CJK ranges `\\u3400-\\u4dbf`, `\\u4e00-\\u9fff`),\nand only for the warning.\n\n## Before F6 starts\n\n- **There is no F6 issue yet.** When it exists, leave a pointer on it to the prototype branch and to this\n  handoff (the prototype process requires it), and put the decision above into its acceptance criteria.\n- **`CONTEXT.md` conflicts with F6.** Under **Review note** it says \"comments stay on Bilibili\". Update the\n  glossary when F6 is specced: add Comment, Comment language and Moderation queue, and fix that line.\n  `CONTEXT.md` avoids \"approve\" for Guides (it uses Publish); decide whether a comment is \"approved\" or uses\n  another word, and record it, rather than using both.\n- **Switch branches first.** This worktree is on `prototype-comment-language`. Go back to the usual branch\n  (or the branch P1 calls for) before writing F6 code, so no F6 work lands on the prototype branch.\n- **Phase label:** `.claude/state/phase` says `P2`. Change it to `F6` when F6 starts.\n\n## Not committed yet\n\n- This handoff (the user asked to keep it uncommitted for now).\n- Transcript 29 (`docs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_29_nick-chen_20261018.md`) and this session's\n  rows in `notes/usage-ledger.csv`.\n\nThese are uncommitted working-tree changes, so they follow the branch switch. They go into the next\n\"Add transcripts …, and update usage ledger\" commit on the usual branch, after the credential scan.\n\n## Working conventions\n\nHandoffs 01–10 (`docs/handoff/`) still hold: the credential scan, the PR flow (never `--delete-branch`), how\nto stop `cms serve`, `rtk proxy` for missing `gh` output, `/browse` instead of the `mcp__claude-in-chrome__*`\ntools, no `Backend:` trailer, and stale manual deadlines (don't flag anything as overdue). `/handoff` is still\nnot in the skill list; read\n`~/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/mattpocock-skills/1.3.1/skills/productivity/handoff/SKILL.md`\nby hand and save to `docs/handoff/`.\n\n## Suggested skills\n\n- `mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling`: add the comment terms to `CONTEXT.md` and fix the Bilibili line.\n- `mattpocock-skills:tdd`: build F6 test-first, starting with the language rules above.\n- `mattpocock-skills:code-review`: before the commit that closes the F6 ticket; post the findings on the issue.\n- `mattpocock-skills:pr`: the P1 pull request carrying F6.\n- `/handoff` (read by hand, as above): at the end of the session.\n"}},"apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CgAvv5vSvMq68fcnZALPA","requestRef":"f7e7f531-c0cc-4e07-b6bf-af66d0b147e4","requestedModel":"claude-opus-5-5","type":"assistant","uuid":"87cd0308-0184-4a5a-9877-e8ed6c4147c9","timestamp":"2026-10-19T04:02:38.856Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"high","perTurnEffort":"high","session_id":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2","sessionId":"5ed2ddc7-3522-4aeb-97a0-6016d4f8d6e4","version":"2.1.303","gitBranch":"prototype-comment-language"}

Handoff: 大巴扎攻略站 CMS, after the P2 prototype (comment language) and before F6\n\nRepo: `/Users/yining/orca/workspaces/iphs400-mp2-cms/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2` (a git worktree). This session\nran on the throwaway branch `prototype-comment-language`, not on the usual `Avulpix0412/IPHS400-Mini-Project-2`.\nExercise order and points are in `notes/mp2plus-plan.md`: P2 is done; F6 is next.\n\n## The prototype (P2)\n\n- **Question:** F6 shows an approved comment only on the page matching its language. Does the rule \"a comment\n  containing any Chinese character is Chinese, otherwise English\" put each comment on the right page? It rests\n  on the claim that English writers in this community never use Chinese characters.\n- **What was tried:** branch `prototype-comment-language`, commit `c335771`, pushed with `-u`\n  (https://github.com/Avulpix0412/iphs400-mp2-cms/tree/prototype-comment-language). One throwaway script,\n  `app/prototype_comment_language.py` (run: `python3 app/prototype_comment_language.py`), checks the rule on\n  16 sample comments: pure Chinese, Chinese with English game terms, pure English, and Chinese internet-style\n  comments with no Chinese characters. The branch is out of `main` and must never be merged.\n- **Finding:** 9 of 16 right. All 7 misses were Chinese internet-style comments with no Chinese characters\n  (`666`, `yyds`, `GG`, `6.7??`, `xswl`, `😂😂😂`, `👍👍`), which the rule sent to the English page. Every comment\n  that did contain a Chinese character really was Chinese (6 of 6), so that half of the rule is always right.\n  The other half (\"no Chinese characters means English\") is not.\n- **A wrong turn, corrected by the user:** I first proposed taking the language from the page the comment was\n  posted on, then a commenter-chosen language. Both assumed a public comment form. **F6 has no public comment\n  form:** an Admin adds comments in the Moderation queue, so there is no page language to start from.\n\n## The decision (the user's)\n\n- The **Admin picks the comment's language** (`zh` or `en`) when adding it. The default is **Chinese**, because\n  most readers are Chinese.\n- If the Admin picks English but the comment contains a Chinese character, **show a warning and keep the\n  Admin's choice**. The detection is only a warning, never an override.\n- The **server accepts only `zh` or `en`** and refuses anything else.\n- **No editing after approval.** Editing comments is out of F6's scope, so a wrong language cannot be fixed\n  after approval.\n- **An English comment on a Guide with no English text is refused with a message.**\n\n## What was carried back\n\nThe decision will land in the F6 comments code. Path: _to be filled in after F6 merges_. Nothing from the\nprototype script is reused except the Chinese-character test (CJK ranges `\\u3400-\\u4dbf`, `\\u4e00-\\u9fff`),\nand only for the warning.\n\n## Before F6 starts\n\n- **There is no F6 issue yet.** When it exists, leave a pointer on it to the prototype branch and to this\n  handoff (the prototype process requires it), and put the decision above into its acceptance criteria.\n- **`CONTEXT.md` conflicts with F6.** Under **Review note** it says \"comments stay on Bilibili\". Update the\n  glossary when F6 is specced: add Comment, Comment language and Moderation queue, and fix that line.\n  `CONTEXT.md` avoids \"approve\" for Guides (it uses Publish); decide whether a comment is \"approved\" or uses\n  another word, and record it, rather than using both.\n- **Switch branches first.** This worktree is on `prototype-comment-language`. Go back to the usual branch\n  (or the branch P1 calls for) before writing F6 code, so no F6 work lands on the prototype branch.\n- **Phase label:** `.claude/state/phase` says `P2`. Change it to `F6` when F6 starts.\n\n## Not committed yet\n\n- This handoff (the user asked to keep it uncommitted for now).\n- Transcript 29 (`docs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_29_nick-chen_20261018.md`) and this session's\n  rows in `notes/usage-ledger.csv`.\n\nThese are uncommitted working-tree changes, so they follow the branch switch. They go into the next\n\"Add transcripts …, and update usage ledger\" commit on the usual branch, after the credential scan.\n\n## Working conventions\n\nHandoffs 01–10 (`docs/handoff/`) still hold: the credential scan, the PR flow (never `--delete-branch`), how\nto stop `cms serve`, `rtk proxy` for missing `gh` output, `/browse` instead of the `mcp__claude-in-chrome__*`\ntools, no `Backend:` trailer, and stale manual deadlines (don't flag anything as overdue). `/handoff` is still\nnot in the skill list; read\n`~/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/mattpocock-skills/1.3.1/skills/productivity/handoff/SKILL.md`\nby hand and save to `docs/handoff/`.\n\n## Suggested skills\n\n- `mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling`: add the comment terms to `CONTEXT.md` and fix the Bilibili line.\n- `mattpocock-skills:tdd`: build F6 test-first, starting with the language rules above.\n- `mattpocock-skills:code-review`: before the commit that closes the F6 ticket; post the findings on the issue.\n- `mattpocock-skills:pr`: the P1 pull request carrying F6.\n- `/handoff` (read by hand, as above): at the end of the session.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}]}}
